## 9.3.1 R2D signals, channels, waveform and procedures

9 Release 20 NR › 9.3 Solutions for Ambient IoT (Internet of Things) in NR Phase 2

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | The chip duration determination of data payload is<br>- Option 2: indicated by the corresponding L1 R2D control information | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605362](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605362.zip) |
| Agreement | For L1 R2D control information preceding scheduled data payload, the chip duration determination of L1 R2D control information is<br>- Option 5: Use periodic broadcast information carried in data payload to indicate the chip duration<br>  - FFS: detail of indication<br>- No use of CAP for chip duration determination of PRDCH<br>- Note: “scheduled data payload” includes all cases other than periodic broadcast information | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605362](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605362.zip) |
| Agreement | For L1 R2D control information preceding periodic broadcast information (if supported), at least the following contents are included<br>- TBS of subsequent periodic broadcast information payload<br>- Transmission parameters for subsequent periodic broadcast information<br>  - FEC, repetition and chip duration (M value)<br>    - FFS: Whether separate or joint indication is needed<br>- FFS: other contents, e.g.,<br>  - [Presence of aperiodic CFO calibration signal after the data payload]<br>  - [(part of) Physical layer Reader ID]<br>  - [Message type]<br>  - [Time duration of CFO calibration signal]<br>- FFS: Content structure and payload size | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605362](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605362.zip) |
| Agreement | For R2D periodic transmission of broadcast information, for single-step acquisition (if supported), deprioritize the following<br>- Alt1: there is no L1 R2D control information preceding the transmission of broadcast information (if supported) | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605363](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605363.zip) |
| Agreement | For R2D periodic transmission of broadcast information, RAN1 select following design<br>  - The periodic broadcast information is transmitted in two parts<br>  - The first part of broadcast information is transmitted with<br>    - Transmission periodicity: same as Psync<br>    - Time resource: immediately after a R2D periodic sync signal transmission including padding if any<br>    - Transmission parameters: pre-defined<br>    - The content of the first part at least includes<br>      - (part of) physical layer Reader ID<br>      - Transmission parameters of the remaining part<br>      - FFS: other content of the first part<br>      - Note: the content structure of the first part is same between the following cases<br>        - The first part is preceding the remaining part of broadcast information<br>        - The first part is not preceding the remaining part of broadcast information<br>  - The remaining part of broadcast information is transmitted with<br>    - Transmission periodicity: integer multiple (>1) of Psync<br>    - Time resource: immediately after a first part transmission including padding if any<br>    - Transmission parameters: indicated by the first part<br>  - Note: the time location where periodic sinusoid single-tone CFO calibration signal is transmitted can be further discussed | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605363](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605363.zip) |
| Agreement | For L1 R2D control information preceding scheduled data payload, at least the following contents are carried<br>- TBS (same as Rel-19)<br>- Transmission parameters for subsequent data payload<br>  - FEC, repetition and chip duration (M value)<br>    - FFS: Whether separate or joint indication is needed<br>- Presence of aperiodic CFO calibration signal after the data payload<br>- (part of) Physical layer Reader ID<br>- Device ID for device monitoring unicast transmission, or pre-defined ID for device monitoring non-unicast transmission e.g. paging<br>- FFS: other contents, e.g.,<br>  - [Message type]<br>  - [Time duration of CFO calibration signal]<br>- FFS: Content structure and payload size<br>  - Whether some content needs to be carried in CRC mask | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605363](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605363.zip) |
| Agreement | For R2D periodic sync signal and sequence-based SIP,<br>  - For R2D periodic sync signal<br>    - The number of binary sequence(s) is 3<br>    - Manchester coding is applied to binary sequence<br>  - For sequence-based SIP<br>    - The number of binary sequence(s) is 3 for a give length<br>    - (Working assumption) Manchester coding is not applied to binary sequence | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605364](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605364.zip) |
| Agreement | For R2D periodic sync signal, a pre-defined sequence length is 255 | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605364](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605364.zip) |
| Agreement | For R2D sequence-based SIP, the maximum sequence length is 511 (based on working assumption) | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605364](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605364.zip) |
| Agreement | For R2D periodic transmission of broadcast information,<br>- The transmission periodicity of the remaining part of broadcast information is indicated by the first part of broadcast information<br>  - Multiple integer values are supported, with a maximum value of 16<br>    - FFS: detail values | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605364](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605364.zip) |
| Agreement | For R2D, for the transmission of periodic unmodulated sinusoid single tone CFO calibration signal<br>- The periodicity is to be selected from the followings<br>  - Option P1: same as the pre-defined transmission periodicity (Psync) of R2D periodic sync signal<br>  - Option P2: integer multiple (>1) of Psync and same as transmission periodicity of the remaining part of broadcast information<br>- The time location(s) is to be selected from the followings<br>  - Option T1: immediately after a periodic R2D sync signal transmission including padding if any<br>  - Option T2: immediately after a first part of periodic broadcast information transmission including padding if any<br>  - Option T3: immediately after a remaining part of periodic broadcast information transmission including padding if any | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605364](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605364.zip) |
| Agreement | For the time duration of unmodulated sinusoid single tone CFO calibration signal transmission, at least 14 OFDM symbols duration including CP is selected<br>- FFS other duration if necessary<br>- Note: the time duration does not include time gap placed at the start and at the end if any | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605364](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605364.zip) |
| Agreement | For R2D, the repetition number(s) are {1, 2, 4} | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605365](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605365.zip) |
| Agreement | For R2D sequence-based SIP,<br>- The sequence length is determined from {511, 255, 127, 63, 31}<br>- The chip length is determined from two or three M values of {2, [6, 12]}<br>  - Device obtains sequence length and M value from periodic broadcast information, the followings are to be further selected<br>    - Option 1: Periodic broadcast information indicates one sequence length and one M value per RSRP range<br>    - Option 2: Periodic broadcast information indicates one sequence length and one M value<br>- FFS: supported combination between sequence length and chip length | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605365](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605365.zip) |
| Agreement | From device perspective, RAN1 to further investigate the potential collision handling if any is needed at least for the following cases, and identify potential solutions if any is needed<br>- Between periodic R2D reception and non-periodic D2R transmission<br>- Between non-periodic R2D reception and periodic D2R transmission resource/occasion (e.g., for the first D2R transmission for DO-A)<br>- Between periodic D2R transmission resource/occasion (e.g., for the first D2R transmission for DO-A) and non-periodic D2R transmission | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605365](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605365.zip) |

| Agreement | For PDRCH scrambling sequence initialization, Device ID-related information is used for cinit calculation except for Msg1 in CBRA and first D2R transmission for DO-A.<br>- For Msg1 in CBRA and first D2R transmission for DO-A, the device uses 0 as the device ID-related information input. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605812](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605812.zip) |
| Agreement | For PDRCH scrambling sequence initialization, Device ID-related information refers to the AS ID defined in TS 38.391, i.e., the AS ID used to address a specific device for R2D reception and D2R scheduling. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605812](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605812.zip) |
| Agreement | Regarding when to (re)initialize the scrambling sequence generator for PDRCH, the scrambling sequence generator is initialized at the beginning of the PDRCH, and a scrambling sequence is generated over the entire PDRCH transmission. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605812](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605812.zip) |
| Agreement | For Rel-20 D2R preamble, determine the supported sequence lengths from {7, 31, 63, 127, 255} based on the following alternatives:<br>- Alt 1: All 5 sequence lengths are supported.<br>- Alt 2: 4 sequence lengths are supported.<br>  - Alt 2-1: Removal of 7<br>  - Alt 2-2: Removal of 127<br>  - Alt 2-3: Modulation-dependent removal of one sequence length, if applicable. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605812](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605812.zip) |
| Agreement | For Rel-20 D2R midamble structure for BPSK, Option 2 structure is used as baseline, while Option 1 structure can be additionally enabled or disabled by the reader via D2R scheduling information.<br>- Option 1: The entire generated midamble sequence is inserted at each midamble position.<br>- Option 2: The generated midamble sequence is divided, and one bit of the sequence is inserted at each midamble position.<br>- FFS: midamble at the end of the PDRCH | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605812](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605812.zip) |
| Agreement | For D2R FDM(A), Option 1, where the D2R frequency resource is determined based on a reference frequency (fref), an offset (foffset) and Tb, is supported. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605812](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605812.zip) |
| Agreement | For determination of the reference frequency (fref), companies are encouraged to provide a comparison between Alt 1 and Alt 2 considering the following aspects:<br>- For Alt 1,<br>  - Alt 1-1: the reference frequency indicated by the reader is based on NREF (i.e., AIoT-ARFCN) defined in TS 38.191 Clause 5.4.1.1, requiring X bits for indication.<br>  - Alt 1-2: the reference frequency indicated by the reader is based on D2R channel raster according to RAN4 discussion, requiring Y bits for indication.<br>- For Alt 2,<br>  - fR2D_sync_raster ​is determined by the device from the received R2D synchronization signal.<br>  - foffset,2 ​has a range of [A, B] MHz and requires C bits for indication to the device.<br>    - FFS: A, B, C, including whether A, B are band-dependent. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605812](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605812.zip) |
| Agreement | The maximum number of D2R block-level repetitions is 16. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605812](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605812.zip) |
| Agreement | For Rel-20 D2R preamble, three sequences are supported for each preamble length. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605812](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605812.zip) |
| Agreement | The modulation scheme is determined based on device type.<br>- Device 2b uses OOK modulation for D2R transmission.<br>- Device C uses BPSK modulation for D2R transmission.<br>- Note: RAN1 strives to minimize the design differences due to OOK and BPSK for D2R. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605813](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605813.zip) |
| Conclusion | For PDRCH scrambling sequence initialization, time-varying information is not included in cinit. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605813](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605813.zip) |
| Agreement | For the initial offset Nc of the scrambling sequence generator, update the agreement as follows.<br>\| Agreement: (RAN1#125) For the initial offset Nc of the scrambling sequence generator, Nc = 1600 as in NR FFS: whether additional specification support (e.g., with limiting number of sequences) is needed to reduce the device complexity \| | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605813](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605813.zip) |
| Agreement | Regarding the applicability of PDRCH scrambling, the agreement is updated as follows:<br>\| Agreement: PDRCH scrambling is applied to all PDRCH transmissions FFS: DO-A scheduling request \| | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605813](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605813.zip) |
| Agreement | For D2R block-level repetition, support one of the following:<br>- Alt 1: {1, 2, 4, 16}<br>- Alt 2: {1, 2, 4, 8, 16}<br>- Alt 3: {1, 2, 8, 16}<br>- Alt 4: {1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 16} | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605813](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605813.zip) |
| Agreement | For D2R preamble sequence generation, companies are encouraged to evaluate different polynomials and initial states for a given preamble sequence length, and RAN1 should strive to down-select at RAN1#126bis:<br>- The preamble sequence is based on an m-sequence.<br>- The companies can refer the following email discussion collecting polynomials and initial states. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605813](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605813.zip) |
| Agreement | For Rel-20 D2R midamble structure, the following alternative is selected for OOK:<br>- Alt 1: Option 1 structure only | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605813](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605813.zip) |
| Agreement | For D2R FDM(A) Option 1, funit is based on the occupied bandwidth. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605813](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605813.zip) |
| Agreement | For D2R bit duration, consider following options.<br>- Option 1: the bit duration set {266.67, 133.33, 66.67, 33.33, 16.67, 8.33, 4.17, 1.39} us is used.<br>- Option 2: the bit duration set {266.67, 133.33, 66.67, 33.33, 16.67, 8.33, 4.17, 2.78, 1.39} us is used.<br>- Option 3: the bit duration set {266.67, 133.33, 66.67, 33.33, 16.67, 8.33, 4.17, 2.78, 1.39} us is used.<br>- Option 4: Option 1 for device 2b and Option 3 for device C. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605814](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605814.zip) |
| Agreement | For D2R FDM(A) Option 1, down-select one of the following:<br>- Alt 1. The offset index k is positive and odd (i.e., k = 1, 3, 5, 7, ...) and funit is half of the occupied bandwidth.<br>  - FFS: k can be even number<br>- Alt 2. The offset index k can be zero, positive and negative and funit is half of the occupied bandwidth.<br>  - k is odd (i.e., k = ..., -5, -3, -1, 1, 3, 5, ...) when the number of FDM(A) resources is even.<br>  - k is even (i.e., k = ..., -4, -2, 0, 2, 4, ...) when the number of FDM(A) resources is odd. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605815](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605815.zip) |
| Agreement | For the D2R midamble insertion structure Option 2, update the agreement below as follows:<br>\| Agreement: (…) For Rel-20 D2R midamble, the following details are assumed under the insertion structure Option 2: Inserted length per midamble occasion: Alt 1. 1 bit Alt 2. 2 bits Base sequence type and length Alt. 1: Same as the preamble (i.e., m-sequence) FFS: If the total number of required midamble bits across the PDRCH exceeds the sequence length, the sequence is repeatedly mapped. Alt. 2: Hadamard sequence FFS: sequence length: {4, 8, 16} FFS: If the total number of required midamble bits across the PDRCH exceeds the sequence length, the sequence is repeatedly mapped. Alt. 3: All 1 sequence A sequence consisting entirely of '1's mapped to all required midamble occasions Note: Companies are encouraged to discuss whether inter-reader midamble interference needs to be considered and whether it can be mitigated by the choice of base sequence type. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605815](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605815.zip) |
| Agreement | For the D2R midamble for BPSK, the candidate insertion intervals for the Option 2 structure are {S/4, S/2, S, S×2, S×4, S×6, S×8} bits, where S is a scaling factor depending on the bit duration (i.e., S = 266.67/Tb).<br>- FFS: Whether further down-selection among the above values and/or additional value(s) is needed. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605815](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605815.zip) |
| Agreement | For the D2R midamble for BPSK, the insertion interval of the Option 1 structure is defined as N times the insertion interval of the Option 2 structure.<br>- FFS: Value(s) of N.<br>- FFS: Handling of the Option 1 and Option 2 structures when their insertion positions coincide.<br>- Note: Option 2 structure is inserted first, and then Option 1 structure is inserted if applicable | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605815](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605815.zip) |
| Agreement | If Alt 1 is supported, for all DO-A, DT, and DO-DTT traffic, fref​ for D2R transmission is indicated via periodic broadcast information (i.e., Alt 1-1). | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605815](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605815.zip) |

## 9.3.3 Other procedures

9 Release 20 NR › 9.3 Solutions for Ambient IoT (Internet of Things) in NR Phase 2

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | Regarding timing related information to be included in periodic broadcast information, support introducing broadcast information index.<br>- For single-step broadcast information acquisition (if supported),<br>  - Alt.1) the broadcast information index is included in L1 control of the broadcast information (if supported)<br>  - Alt.2) the broadcast information index is included in data payload of the broadcast information.<br>  - Alt.3) part of the broadcast information index is included in the L1 control of the broadcast information (if supported) and the remaining part in the data payload of the broadcast information<br>- For two-step broadcast information acquisition (if supported),<br>  - Alt.1) the broadcast information index is included in the MIB-like broadcast information<br>  - Alt.2) the broadcast information index is included in the SIB1-like broadcast information<br>  - Alt.3) part of the broadcast information index is included in the MIB-like broadcast information and the remaining part in the SIB1-like broadcast information<br>- FFS: value range of the index<br>- FFS: when/how to use the timing related information as timing reference for subsequent R2D/D2R transmissions | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606198](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606198.zip) |
| Agreement | For the broadcast information index to be included in periodic broadcast information, consider following factors for the determination of value range of the broadcast information index:<br>- 1) Modification period (if supported)<br>- 2) Paging monitoring cycle<br>- 3) Periodicity for periodic DO-A data transfer (based on TR 22.840)<br>- Other factors are not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606198](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606198.zip) |

| Agreement | Confirm the following working assumption with following update.<br>\| Working assumption: RAN1#125 For one or multiple D2R resource set(s) provided by a periodic broadcast information A periodic broadcast information provides one or multiple “resource set configuration(s)” Each “resource set configuration” configures multiple resource sets that are periodic or a single resource set. At least the following configuration parameter can be different among the multiple “resource set configurations” Periodicity of resource set Time location of resource set Note: the content structure of the resource set configuration is same between the case of a single resource set and the case of multiple resource sets \| | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606199](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606199.zip) |

| Agreement | Regarding timing related information to be included in periodic broadcast information, support introducing broadcast information index.<br>- For single-step broadcast information acquisition (if supported),<br>  - Alt.1) the broadcast information index is included in L1 control of the broadcast information (if supported)<br>  - Alt.2) the broadcast information index is included in data payload of the broadcast information.<br>  - Alt.3) part of the broadcast information index is included in the L1 control of the broadcast information (if supported) and the remaining part in the data payload of the broadcast information<br>- For two-step broadcast information acquisition (if supported),<br>- On where the broadcast information index is included,<br>  - Alt.1) the broadcast information index is included in the MIB-like first part of broadcast information<br>  - Alt.2) the broadcast information index is included in the SIB1-like remaining part of broadcast information<br>  - Alt.3) part of the broadcast information index is included in the MIB-like first part of broadcast information and the remaining part of the broadcast information index is in the SIB1-like remaining part of broadcast information<br>- FFS: whether index corresponds to the first part or the remaining part, and value range of the broadcast information index according to the selected alternative<br>- FFS: when/how to use the timing related information as timing reference for subsequent R2D/D2R transmissions<br>--- | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606201](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606201.zip) |
| Agreement | For initial physical layer Reader ID-related information acquisition, part of the physical layer Reader ID-related information is indicated by periodic sync signal sequences using 3 sequences and the remaining part of the physical layer Reader ID-related information is indicated by the first part of periodic broadcast information. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606201](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606201.zip) |
| Agreement | For collision handling between Uu and A-IoT at an intermediate UE in D2T2, RAN1 to consider at least following options as a general principle for collision handling b/w Uu and A-IoT if needed:<br>- Opt.1) Intermediate UE prioritizes Uu over A-IoT.<br>- Opt.2) Intermediate UE prioritizes A-IoT over Uu.<br>- Opt.3) gNB avoids collision b/w Uu and A-IoT.<br>- Opt.4) Up to UE to implementation.<br>- Note: Different options for different cases are not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606201](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606201.zip) |
| Conclusion | It is up to reader implementation to additionally use D2R midamble and/or PDRCH for D2R RSRP-like measurement for device localization. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606201](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606201.zip) |
| Agreement | For R2D RSRP measurements at the device, down select from following alternatives<br>- Alt.1: at least periodic R2D sync signal is used.<br>  - It is up to device implementation to use other signal/channel e.g., the first part of the periodic R2D broadcast information<br>- Alt.2: periodic R2D sync signal and the first part of the periodic R2D broadcast information are used. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606201](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606201.zip) |

## 9.4.1 NR-NTN GNSS resilience

9 Release 20 NR › 9.4 GNSS resilient NR-NTN operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For open-loop UL time/frequency pre-compensation on the service link in (quasi) Earth-fixed cells, for initial access support a reference-location-based approach together with satellite ephemeris where;<br>- For one SSB beam per cell, cell specific reference location is supported.<br>- For multiple SSB beams per cell, SSB beam specific reference location is supported.<br>  - The association between reference location and the applicable SSB beam should be defined<br>  - FFS: Whether cell specific reference location could be configured in this case<br>- FFS: Whether/how to support multiple reference locations for each SSB beam<br>Note: Signalling details will be discussed in a separate proposal | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605606](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605606.zip) |
| Agreement | For open-loop UL time/frequency pre-compensation on the service link in Earth-moving cells, for initial access support a common TA/Doppler-information-based approach where;<br>- For one SSB beam per cell, cell specific common TA/Doppler on the service link is supported.<br>- For multiple SSB beams per cell, SSB beam specific common TA/Doppler on the service link is supported.<br>  - The association between common TA/Doppler-information and the applicable SSB beam should be defined.<br>  - FFS: Whether cell specific service link common TA/Doppler could be configured in this case<br>- FFS: Whether/how to support multiple common TA/Doppler on the service link for each SSB beam<br>- Note: Signalling details will be discusses in a separate proposal | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605606](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605606.zip) |
| Agreement | For frequency adjustment command (FAC) support in Msg2/RAR, RAN1 is to consider the following aspects:<br>- defining the FAC range to address at least the largest residual frequency errors reported in TR 38.742;<br>- defining the FAC granularity;<br>- supporting negative/positive FAC.<br>It is up to RAN2 to decide whether a new MAC RAR is needed to carry the FAC, or whether the current RAR structure can be reused with extensions.<br>The range/granularity values and the unit are discussed separately.<br>The indication of TAC and FAC (whether jointly or not) is discussed separately. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605606](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605606.zip) |
| Agreement | For TA command in Msg2/RAR, RAN1 to consider the following enhancements:<br>- Extending TAC range, in order to address at least largest residual timing errors reported in TR 38.742.<br>- Keep the current TA granularity as the baseline assumption<br>- Either negative/positive TAC or mitigated via higher layer configured/predefined TA offset that UE applies for all transmissions<br>It is up to RAN2 to decide whether a new MAC RAR is needed to carry the TAC, or whether the current RAR structure can be reused with extensions.<br>The range/granularity values are discussed separately.<br>Note: The indication of TAC and FAC (whether jointly or not) is discussed separately. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605607](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605607.zip) |
| Agreement | For the solution involving transmission of two PRACH sequences with the same existing PRACH format and different roots, Alt 1-4 in agreement below is adopted as baseline.<br>FFS: Whether same RO and/or separate ROs in time domain can be used to transmit the two PRACH sequences.<br>- Note: The decision will be made next meeting.<br>\| Agreement: Further study the following Solution 1A variants: Variant 1A-1: two PRACH sequences with the same existing PRACH format and different roots, including: Alt 1-1: conjugate-root sequences transmitted in different RACH occasions / consecutive ROs, Alt 1-2: conjugate-root sequences transmitted at the same time within one RO, overlapping in time and frequency, Alt 1-3: two-root transmission with phase shift / PSK-based differentiation by pre-compensation applied to the second sequence, Alt 1-4: two-root transmission where the two roots are not necessarily conjugate; Variant 1A-2: two PRACH sequences using different PRACH formats (e.g. long PRACH followed by short PRACH). Variant 1A-3: multiple PRACH sequences using multiple reference locations or common TAs/Dopplers on service link \| | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605609](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605609.zip) |
| Working Assumption | RAN1 to consider the following WFs for down selection at next meeting:<br>WF1: The granularity for frequency adjustment command is defined according to: . The value of k is to be defined. As a starting point for evaluation, k is set to be equal to:<br>- Alt 1: k =10<br>- Alt 2: k= 8<br>- Alt 3: k= 6<br>- Other value may be considered<br>WF2: The granularity for frequency adjustment command is defined according to:<br>- Alt 1: 0.01 ppm<br>- Alt 2: 0.1 ppm<br>- Alt 3: 0.05 ppm<br>- Other value may be considered<br>FFS: Whether one or multiple granularities will be defined<br>Note: the above applies to both initial access and connected mode<br>Note: the SCS in WF1 is the SCS provided in the uplink BWP config<br>Note: The combination of above WFs is not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605609](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605609.zip) |

## 10.4.1 Operation in idle mode

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.4 Multiple carrier operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For further study of initial access for idle mode multi-carrier operation, consider at least the following potential use cases:<br>  - UC-1: UL coverage enhancement / DL-UL coverage imbalance<br>  - UC-2: RACH capacity / offloading / load balancing<br>  - UC-3: Network energy saving / common-signaling reduction<br>  - Other use cases are not precluded.<br>  - For each identified use case, study whether and under which conditions multiple-carrier operation in idle mode provides additional benefit over existing mechanisms. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in idle mode]` |
| Agreement | For study of multiple carriers where for initial access a UE can utilize resources for UL (Msg1, etc) or for both UL (Msg1, etc.) and DL (Msg2, etc.) in one carrier other than the carrier where the UE obtains the system information providing configuration for those resources, consider the following potential scenarios for the set of candidate physical carriers for which resources are provided for initial access:<br>  - S1: 1 DL physical carrier + N UL physical carriers, where N > 1<br>  - S2: M DL physical carriers + 1 UL physical carrier, where M > 1<br>  - S3: M DL physical carriers + N UL physical carriers, where M > 1 and N > 1<br>Note 1: The carrier where the UE obtains the system information and other candidate physical carrier(s) are counted above.<br>Note 2: For a given UE and a given initial access procedure, UE may use only a subset of the candidate physical carrier.<br>Note 3: The inclusion of a scenario for study does not imply agreement to support it. The need and applicable use cases/deployment scenarios for each scenario are subject to further study. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in idle mode]` |
| Agreement | For study of multiple carriers where for initial access a UE can utilize resources for UL (Msg1, etc) or for both UL (Msg1, etc.) and DL (Msg2, etc.) in one carrier other than the carrier where the UE obtains the system information providing configuration for those resources,<br>  - As a baseline, a single selected physical carrier is used per link direction throughout an attempt of an RACH procedure. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in idle mode]` |
| Agreement | For further study of multiple carriers where for initial access a UE can utilize resources for UL (Msg1, etc) or for both UL (Msg1, etc.) and DL (Msg2, etc.) in one carrier other than the carrier where the UE obtains the system information providing configuration for those resources, consider at least the following deployment conditions:<br>  - D1: co-located carriers for which a common timing/frequency synchronization relationship can be assumed<br>  - D2: co-located carriers for which a common timing/frequency synchronization relationship cannot be assumed<br>  - D3: non-collocated carriers for which a common timing/frequency synchronization relationship cannot be assumed<br>  - Other relevant deployment assumptions, including carrier frequency relationship / band separation, numerology / duplexing configuration and UE RF/baseband capability, are considered as applicable.<br>Note: whether a common timing/frequency synchronization relationship can be assumed is from UE perspective. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in idle mode]` |

| Agreement | For 6GR multiple carrier operation in connected mode, study mechanisms for fast enabling/disabling access to additional carriers, considering at least the following:<br>- UE power saving and/or NW energy saving<br>- Carrier states<br>- How to enable/disable<br>- Assumed NW-side requirements/conditions<br>- Assumed UE-side requirements | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in connected mode]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR multiple carrier operation in connected mode, study mechanisms for PXSCH resource allocation, considering at least the following:<br>- A PXSCH confined within a carrier<br>- A PXSCH over multiple carriers<br>  - Applicable scenario and condition<br>  - Coordination with other WG(s), e.g., RAN4 (as needed) | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in connected mode]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR multiple carrier operation in connected mode, study mechanisms for scheduling PXSCH, considering at least the following:<br>- Self-carrier scheduling<br>  - Note: self-carrier scheduling is that a DCI on a carrier schedules a PXSCH on a same carrier<br>- Cross-carrier scheduling<br>  - Note: cross-carrier scheduling is that a DCI on a carrier schedules a PXSCH on another carrier<br>- Multi-carrier scheduling<br>  - Note: multi-carrier scheduling is that a DCI on a carrier schedules a single PXSCH across multiple carriers, or individual PXSCH per carrier<br>- Coordination with other WG(s), e.g., RAN4 (as needed)<br>- Applicable scenario and condition for the above mechanism(s)<br>- Multiple slot scheduling will be discussed separately in other agenda. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in connected mode]` |
| Agreement | For the study of bandwidth operation with single carrier operation in CONNECTED mode for a UE in 6G, at least consider the following:<br>- A “6GR-BW” should comprise at least a frequency location and the number of contiguous PRBs within a carrier.<br>  - Study the necessity that any other parameter (s) are associated to a “6GR-BW” operation<br>    - Note: RAN1 strives to minimize the number of parameter(s) that is associated with a “6GR-BW”<br>- Signal(s)/channel(s) transmission/receiving associated to a “6GR-BW”<br>- Benefit(s) and necessity of switching of “6GR-BW” for DL and/or UL, and potential mechanisms for switching | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in connected mode]` |

| Agreement | For the link budget evaluations in around 7 GHz for coverage gap identification, the assumptions for the receiver noise figure are 9 dB and 5 dB for DL and UL respectively (agreed in RAN1#124)<br>- Send an LS to RAN4 about the assumptions for information only. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |

| Agreement | For SSB transmission, study<br>Option 1:<br>For a given frequency band, a single set of values for {X, Y, M} is predefined in default 6GR SSB periodicity<br>- Non-adjustable number of repetitions of transmitted SSBs<br>Note: for discussion purpose,<br>- X: the max number of candidate 6GR SSBs in a 6GR SSB burst<br>- Y: the max number of candidate 6GR SSB burst(s) in a default 6GR SSB periodicity<br>- M: the max number of candidate 6GR SSB repetitions within a default 6GR SSB periodicity<br>Option 2:<br>For a given frequency band, a single set of values for {X, Y, M} is predefined in default 6GR SSB periodicity and the actual number of repetitions of transmitted SSBs can be adjusted by the aNB<br>- FFS: how to adjust the actual number of repetitions of transmitted SSBs including potential restrictions<br>Note: for discussion purpose,<br>- X: the max number of candidate 6GR SSBs in a 6GR SSB burst<br>- Y: the max number of candidate 6GR SSB burst(s) in a default 6GR SSB periodicity<br>- M: the max number of candidate 6GR SSB repetitions within a default 6GR SSB periodicity | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| Agreement | RAN1 to study and evaluate the following solutions<br>- Option 1:<br>  - SSB repetition within one SSB period and UE is assumed to at least support SSB combining within one SSB period<br>  - 160 ms default SSB periodicity for initial cell search<br>- Option 2:<br>  - SSB repetition within one SSB period and UE is assumed to at least support SSB combining within one SSB period<br>  - 80 ms default SSB periodicity for initial cell search<br>- Option 3:<br>  - UE is assumed to at least support SSB combining across different SSB periods<br>  - 40 ms default SSB periodicity for initial cell search<br>- Option 4:<br>  - SSB repetition within one SSB period and UE is assumed to at least support SSB combining within one SSB period and support SSB combining across different SSB periods<br>  - 40 ms default SSB periodicity for initial cell search<br>- Option 5:<br>  - UE is assumed to at least support SSB combining across different SSB periods<br>  - 20 ms default SSB periodicity for initial cell search<br>To mitigate the UE impact with longer SSB periodicity in Option 1/2/3/4, RAN1 to study the following solutions<br>- Solutions to reduce cell search complexity and latency<br>- Additional synchronization signals<br>- Clustered allocations of SSB, SIB1 and paging<br>- Other solutions are not precluded<br>FFS: Additional configurable SSB periodicities | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| Agreement | Draft LS R1-2606890 is endorsed in principle.<br>Final LS R1-2606891 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |

| Agreement | For the study of preamble format reduction, below template is adopted for companies to provide set of preamble design targets that provide enablement of 6GR use cases and deployment scenarios. Please provide minimal set of preamble design targets that companies believe is the best representation for 6GR, including information on how the representative preamble design targets for 6GR is selected.<br>- A row represented Preamble design targets are represented by {Carrier Frequency, Delay (Max RTT + Delay Spread), Doppler Shift} tuple.<br>\| Identifier (company + Index) \| Carrier Frequency \| Delay (Max RTT + Delay Spread) \| Doppler Shift \| Notes (any information to additional share, e.g., sequence length, occupied BW, SCS, number of sequence repetition, preamble duration, frame structure restrictions, information on derivation, motivation, etc) \|<br>\|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \| | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| Conclusion | To report the following details for PRACH evaluations:<br>- Receiver algorithm details, including:<br>  - Detection threshold strategy:<br>    - threshold configured to address false alarm only (impacting miss detection)<br>      - Example 1) Threshold is determined based on noise variance<br>    - threshold configured to address false alarm (impacting miss detection) and false detection probability jointly<br>      - Example 2) Threshold is determined based on noise variance, inter-sequence interference + noise estimated from measurements outside the window of the detected peak<br>        - Detection metric is normalized by interference+noise estimate, where the estimate is derived (e.g., maximum or median of the detection metric) from the detection metric outside the window around the detected peak within the NCS duration of the preamble. The detection peak window can be +/- estimated/approximated channel delay.<br>      - Example 3) Threshold is determined based on noise variance, sequence interference estimated from measurement of total receive signal power of a RO<br>  - In case of ZC sequence detection with Restricted Set A and B, details of detection algorithm to handle the detection sidelobes.<br>- For False alarm statistics, which option used for false alarm (and determination of detection threshold)<br>  - Option 1) if BS performs detection on a RO, the false alarm event happens for a preamble sequence detection, and a false alarm event is counted for the preamble sequence (false alarm event may happen multiple times within a RO for across the multiple preamble sequences). In this case, the denominator used to compute false alarm probability is the number of preambles to be detected (per RO).<br>  - Option 2) if BS performs detection on a RO, the false alarm event happens for any preamble sequence detection, and the a single false alarm event counted for a RO. In this case, denominator used to compute false alarm probability is 1 (per RO).<br>- Relative Tx power assumption of UEs (and any pathloss measurement and RF impairment modeling, if used)<br>- User distribution per RO, for example<br>  - Same number of users transmitting PRACH preamble in each RO<br>  - Varying number of users transmitting PRACH preamble in each RO (may be based on traffic characteristics)<br>- Preamble sequence selection<br>  - For example, NR ZC provides information on number of root sequences, number of cyclic shifts, and Ncs value among the 64 preamble set used for evaluation. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| Agreement | For study of whether the maximum number of preambles per RO is increased beyond 64, take into account at least:<br>- the justification and use case for the capacity increase (e.g., load model, access rate per cell, target collision probability, availability of ROs in time/frequency);<br>- the available root-sequence pool and the resulting inter-cell reusing for the assumed sequence length; and<br>  - Note: Preamble sequence, format, as well as availability of ROs in time/frequency are taken into account when calculating available sequence pool and reuse.<br>- the resulting Miss detection, Type-1 (intra-cell), and Type-2 (inter-cell) false-detection performance under PRACH transmission load in the network.<br>  - Companies are asked to provide information on detection algorithm and detection metric thresholds (and adaptation strategies, if any) used for evaluations.<br>- associated transmitter/receiver complexity, and random access latency | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |

| Agreement | For initial access in a single-carrier cell,<br>- For option 2, further study<br>  - Alt 1: The schedulable frequency resources of SIB1-PDSCH are predefined without explicit signaling in MIB.<br>  - Alt 2: The schedulable frequency resources of SIB1-PDSCH are explicitly signaled in MIB, subject to minimum/limited impact to MIB coverage/overhead. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| Agreement | Single numerology is adopted for the common RB (CRB) grid for 6GR single-carrier operation for a given band. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.5 Multi-antenna system › 10.5.2 MIMO operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | - At least aggregation levels of 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 are supported for 6GR PDCCH.<br>  - Note: A CCE aggregation level is not used when the number of CCE in a CORESET is smaller than the aggregation level. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606611](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606611.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - A CCE consists of 6 REGs for 6GR PDCCH<br>- REGs in a REG bundle are contiguous in frequency and time.<br>- At least REG-bundle size of 6 REGs is supported<br>  - FFS a need for other sizes. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606612](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606612.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | For the study of precoding granularity consider the following options from the UE perspective:<br>- Option 1: Precoding granularity is only a single REG bundle<br>- Option 2: The same precoder is used for all contiguous REGs in a CORESET<br>- Option 3: The same precoder is used for multiple contiguous REG bundles in time/frequency within the PDCCH candidate<br>- Option 4: The same precoder is used for multiple contiguous REG bundles in time/frequency. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606612](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606612.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - Both contiguous and non-contiguous frequency-domain resource allocation are supported for 6GR CORESET.<br>- At least 6-PRB 6GR PDCCH CORESET frequency-domain resource allocation granularity is supported<br>  - FFS a need, feasibility and conditions for other CORESET granularities | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606613](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606613.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |

| Agreement | Companies are encouraged to provide the analysis for comparison of MU-MIMO schemes (based on orthogonal and/or non-orthogonal DMRS) and single-layer, single-port, single-user PDCCH considering<br>  - The necessity to introduce orthogonal DMRS for 6GR PDCCH<br>  - At least the following two cases:<br>    - Case1: double resources for two paired MU-MIMO UEs than for a single user and the same total power are assumed from NW point of view<br>    - Case 2: the same resources for single user and two paired MU-MIMO users and the same total power are assumed from NW point of view<br>  - Performance based on link level and/or system level evaluations (e.g., BLER, spectrum efficiency)<br>  - Used models and criteria on the user pairing for MU-MIMO based on orthogonal multi-port DMRS and/or non-orthogonal single-port DMRS<br>  - Assumptions on the UE receiver<br>  - Assumptions on UE specific precoding<br>  - Impacts on UE complexity<br>  - Assumed channel model (at least CDL channel model for link level evaluations)<br>  - Assumed DCI size<br>Note: The discussion of the utilization of orthogonal DMRS ports for other purposes (e.g. intercell-interference mitigation) than MU-MIMO is not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606614](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606614.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |

| Agreement | Support both of the following methods to convey the UCI over physical channel:<br>- Method 1: Define a UCI dedicated physical channel, i.e., Physical Uplink Control Channel (PUCCH)<br>- Method 2: UCI carried on PUSCH<br>  - Note: Whether/how to support each UCI type (e.g., HARQ-ACK, CSI) on PUSCH with/without data is up to other agendas<br>Further study on Option 3 per agreement in RAN1#124 | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605830](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605830.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For transmission scheme(s) for 6GR PUCCH formats, study at least the followings aspects:<br>- Scheme(s) with sequence(s) without DMRS<br>- Scheme(s) with DMRS and/or channel coding<br>  - Note: It does not mean that scheme(s) with DMRS only is considered<br>- RAN1 strives for smaller number of formats for 6GR PUCCH, e.g., up to 2 or 3 formats.<br>- Study at least the following point(s) for differentiation among 6GR PUCCH formats<br>  - UCI payload size<br>  - Transmission scheme<br>  - Waveform<br>  - Modulation schemes<br>  - The number of OFDM symbols/PRBs for one PUCCH resource<br>  - Multi-user multiplexing scheme(s)/capability/capacity<br>- The study shall consider NR PUCCH formats as the benchmark | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605830](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605830.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | Support the following for UL DMRS for 6GR PUSCH at least based on CP-OFDM waveform<br>- For X = 2, Nmax = 48<br>- For X = 1, FFS: Nmax where Nmax<= 24<br>- The CDM group structure is comb-based including at least comb-2<br>  - FFS: Applicable cases for comb-2<br>- Note:<br>  - X is the TD-OCC length<br>  - Nmax is the maximum number of orthogonal DMRS ports<br>  - AI/ML based DMRS overhead reduction is a separate discussion<br>- FFS: Maximum number of orthogonal DMRS ports for DFT-s-OFDM | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605830](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605830.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | - For DMRS based on TD-OCC-X for a single-slot based PUSCH, the TD-OCC-X is applied to X consecutive symbols for DMRS<br>  - Consider the same spacing between every X DMRS symbols<br>    - Study how to identify the spacing between every X DMRS symbols<br>    - FFS: whether there is any exception<br>  - The number of DMRS symbols in a slot is L*X, and study the candidate value of L as follows<br>    - For X = 1, L = {1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6}<br>    - For X = 2, L = {1, 2, 3}<br>    - If multiple candidate values of L are supported, study how to determine the value of L<br>  - Study at least the following options for the time-domain location of the first DMRS symbol<br>    - Option 1: The first DMRS symbol is fixed as the first symbol allocated for the PUSCH<br>    - Option 2: The candidate DMRS symbol(s) are fixed in a slot and the actual first DMRS symbol is the first candidate DMRS symbol within the TDRA for the PUSCH<br>    - Option 3: The first DMRS symbol is indicated by the network relative to the first symbol of the PUSCH | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605830](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605830.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR PUSCH transmission, study AI based receiver for PUSCH DMRS overhead reduction at least considering the followings:<br>- Candidate(s) of low overhead DMRS pattern<br>  - Subcase A<br>    - At least Comb-based low overhead DMRS pattern in frequency domain<br>    - FFS: time domain<br>  - Subcase B in power domain<br>Note: For a given low overhead DMRS pattern, it is transparent to the UE the type of receiver  (AI or non-AI) used at the network. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605831](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605831.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |

| Agreement | The following is endorsed  as Draft an LS to RAN4 regarding the 6GR the RF coherence requirements.<br>1 Overall description:<br>RAN1 are studying UL MIMO codebooks for 6G and have reached the following agreements:<br>\| Agreement: For codebook based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, study fixed codebook for full-coherent precoder type considering the following options and aspects: Option 1. Reuse NR UL codebook Option 2. Grassmannian codebook Option 3. Per-element phase/amplitude quantization targeting SVD precoder approximation Option 4. DFT based precoder Other option(s) are not precluded. Consider different antenna architectures and different waveforms Whether/how to separately or commonly design 2TX/3TX/4TX/8TX codebook Whether to keep constant modulus property Whether/how to consider UE PA (power amplifier) constraint Whether/how to define single-layer precoder only, and indicating a combination of precoder(s) Consider different codebook size and precoder indication overhead Whether/how to separately or commonly design codebook for CP-OFDM and DFT-S-OFDM. Note: Partial/non-coherent precoder type are separately discussed. Note: Per-layer power control is separately discussed. Agreement: For evaluation on coherent UL MIMO based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, at least the RF coherence requirements for coherent UL MIMO defined in TS38.101-1/2 are used as baseline Companies to report Other additional RF coherence requirements can be reported by companies (optional) TX coherency error model \|<br>RAN1 are currently using the coherent UL MIMO requirements in TS38.101-1/2 as baselines for evaluations including coherence imperfections. RAN1 would like to know if the /phase errors in these requirements  for 6G, especially for devices that support more than two port UL MIMO transmission.<br>RAN1 have not so far agreed to models of coherence imperfections for codebook evaluations, and so such models are determined by each company. Therefore, while RAN1 can continue investigations, RAN1 would welcome suggestions on /phase error modeling for 6G UEs so that these may be used to refine RAN1’s evaluations.<br>2	Actions<br>To RAN4<br>ACTION: 	RAN1 respectfully asks RAN4 if the /phase errors in current coherent UL MIMO requirements in TS38.101-1/2  for 6G, especially for devices that support more than two port UL MIMO transmission. While RAN1 can continue investigations, RAN1 would also welcome suggestions on /phase error modeling for UL MIMO.<br>The corresponding final LS in R1-2606899 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605832](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605832.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For codebook based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, a fixed codebook is further clarified as follow:<br>- For a given precoder indication size, a set of precoder(s) in fixed codebook is pre-defined/specified in the specification<br>- Note: All 5G NR DL/UL codebooks are fixed codebook.<br>- Note: Per agreement in RAN1#124bis, a fixed codebook is a codebook which is pre-defined in specification. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605832](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605832.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For codebook based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, study configurable codebook considering the following options and aspects:<br>- Option 1. Downloadable codebook<br>  - NW determines a set of precoder(s)/matrices.<br>  - From NW, UE is configured with a set of precoder(s)/matrices.<br>  - After NW acquires UL CSI, an UL precoder within the set of precoder(s) is indicated by NW to UE.<br>- Option 2. Uploadable codebook<br>  - UE determines a set of precoder(s) and reports to NW.<br>  - From NW, UE is configured with a set of precoder(s).<br>  - After NW acquires UL CSI, an UL precoder within the set of precoder(s) is indicated by NW to UE.<br>- Whether to consider constant modulus property<br>- Whether/how to indicate/obtain UE’s relevant information<br>- How to construct hybrid approach between fixed codebook and configurable codebook<br>- Memory burden / implementation complexity for UE/NW side<br>- Applicable scenario/use case (e.g., applicable number of antenna ports)<br>- How to consider PAPR increase and UE power constraint | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605832](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605832.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |

| Agreement | For the 6GR codebook design for CSI report, study a W1W2 only structure for the low-resolution scheme with the following considerations at least for RI=1-4:<br>- W1-structure<br>  - SD basis<br>    - NR oversampled 2D-DFT basis is a baseline<br>      - FFS on other basis, e.g., basis constructed from combined 2D DFT beams<br>      - FFS on alphabet set for combination coefficients for the basis constructed from combined 2D DFT beams<br>  - SD basis selection<br>    - eType-I Scheme-B like unrestricted orthogonal DFT beam selection in 2D direction is a baseline<br>      - FFS on orthogonal basis selection in at least 1D direction, or non-orthogonal basis selection<br>    - Layer-specific/layer-group-specific SD basis selection<br>      - FFS on basis-to-layer (layer-group) mapping, e.g., ordering or no ordering<br>      - FFS on the maximum number of SD basis vector selection<br>- W2-structure<br>  - Layer-specific/layer-group-specific inter-polarization co-phase selection<br>    - FFS: inter-polarization amplitude<br>  - Alphabet set for inter-polarization co-phase: BPSK, QPSK<br>    - FFS: details for each rank<br>    - FFS: for WB, whether to consider or not 8-PSK in addition to BPSK and QPSK<br>- FFS on RI>4<br>- Strive for having a unified codebook scheme for all supported ranks by 6G<br>- FFS: details on whether/how to accommodate passive array with smaller number of ports<br>- Note: UE implementation complexity is to be considered in the design of SD-basis and co-phasing selection, including the number of candidate combinations and the associated search complexity.<br>- Note: CBSR discussion will be discussed separately<br>- Note: For discussion of low resolution scheme, eType-1 Scheme-A and eType-II with L=1 are precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |

| Agreement | For CSI-RS sequence in 6G, take the NR PN sequence and mapping as the baseline for evaluation, further study the following aspects<br>- How to achieve low-PAPR for pre-coded CSI-RS by sequence and/or mapping, compared to NR PN sequence and its mapping<br>- Handle the case with high inter-cell interference /how to handle the case with high inter-cell interference<br>- CSI-RS sharing for different frequency density<br>- On sequence initialization, take NR UE specific configuration for initialization as the baseline, study whether other options are needed | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For 6G CSI-RS, study the following options to address the potential PAPR issue caused by precoded CSI-RS<br>- Option 1: BS implementation-based approach, e.g., use UE-transparent CDM-specific small cyclic shifts, or use small CDM group size<br>- Option 2: Map the PN sequence to the global subcarrier indices in the whole bandwidth sequentially<br>- Option 3: Multiply the PN sequence with an additional CDM group-specific scrambling sequence<br>- Option 4: Multiply the PN sequence with an additional port-specific scrambling sequence<br>- Option 5: Apply segmented phase rotation on the PN sequence | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
