L>1 Layer-common SD basis selection<br>    - eType-II orthogonal DFT beam selection in 2D direction is a baseline<br>      - FFS orthogonal basis selection in at least 1D direction<br>      - FFS non-orthogonal L basis selection, e.g., basis vector- specific (q1, q2) selection<br>      - FFS orthogonal L basis selection, non-orthogonal basis selection across different layers, e.g., orthogonal DFT beam selection in 2D direction for each layer with layer-specific (q1, q2) selection<br>      - FFS whether/how to further optimize SD basis selection, e.g., using layer-common in combination with layer-specific/layer group-specific<br>- FFS whether to consider RI>4<br>- Wf-structure<br>  - eType-II layer-specific FD basis selection as a baseline<br>    - FFS on whether/how to further optimize FD compression, e.g., delay-domain pre-alignment per-basis prior to FD compression<br>- W2-structure<br>  - Linear combining (LC) coefficient scheme (ref/diff amps, phase, SCI) analogous to Rel-16 NR eType-II as a starting point<br>    - FFS: details on alphabet sets on LC coefficients<br>  - Whether to support bitmap for indicating the location of NZCs as well as a parameter for the maximum number of NZCs<br>- FFS parameter combination (PC) design<br>- Note: UE implementation complexity is to be considered in the design of SD-basis selection, including the additional complexity of inter-layer interference mitigation for layer-specific schemes. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR CSI-RS, study CSI-RS design to achieve low CSI-RS overhead considering the following possible directions<br>- Direction 1: Sparse CSI-RS in frequency/spatial domain<br>  - Consider AI and/or non-AI prediction to reconstruct the channel at UE and/or NW side<br>  - Study sparse pattern<br>- Direction 2: Use non-orthogonal CSI-RS ports<br>  - Study the use of cyclic shifts or scrambling sequences across CSI-RS RBs, or non-orthogonal cover code<br>  - Inter CSI-RS port interference leakage is to be considered in the evaluations<br>- Note: Combinations of the directions can be considered.<br>- Note: At least total CSI-RS overhead, performance and UE/NW complexity are to be studied<br>- Note: Companies to report the assumed CSI-RS time-domain behavior including CSI-RS periodicity in the evaluations<br>- Note: companies to report how CSI-RS overhead can be reduced from system perspective | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For evaluation on CPI reporting in SD/FD/TD, study the following options of form of CPI:<br>- Option 1: Channel covariance matrix/spectrum, e.g., ,  in form of basis matrix and associated coefficients and/or power, wherein  is in port/frequency domain<br>  - FFS: Whether orthogonal or non-orthogonal bases are used for compression<br>  - FFS: Whether off-diagonal coefficients in additional to diagonal coefficients of full or subset of<br>  - FFS: Whether CPI in SD/FD/TD domains are reported separately or jointly<br>- Option 2: Channel covariance matrix in form of quantization of decomposed channel covariance matrices, e.g., EVD, QR<br>- Option 3: Normalized correlation<br>  - FFS: whether CPIs in different domains are reported separately or jointly<br>- Option 4: Channel matrix (utilizing the explicit CSI report)<br>- Option 5: Explicit system parameter, e.g., codebook parameter, RS parameter.<br>Companies to provide details of applicable use cases for the above options.<br>Companies report calculation assumption of the channel covariance matrix/spectrum, e.g., average over time domain, average over frequency domain, average over spatial domain.<br>Note: Strive for common report quantities for use cases for a certain domain, if applicable. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For the evaluation of stand-alone reporting for CJT calibration report, consider the following aspects:<br>- Reporting quantities of Rel-19 CJTC-Dd/F/P can be candidates<br>- xTRP delay offset report<br>  - Study dynamic range ADO and resolution MFO<br>    - Rel-19 CJTC-Dd dynamic range and resolution can be considered as a starting point<br>    - FFS: other candidate values of ADO and MDO<br>- xTRP frequency offset report<br>  - Study dynamic range AFO and resolution MFO<br>    - Rel-19 CJTC-F dynamic range and resolution can be considered as a starting point<br>    - FFS: other candidate values of AFO and MFO<br>- xTRP phase offset report<br>  - Study resolution MP and frequency granularity (WB, SB)<br>    - Rel-19 CJTC-P resolution can be considered as a starting point<br>    - FFS: other candidate values of MP<br>- Companies to report how to acquire mTRP CSI for evaluation, e.g., SRS-based CSI acquisition or CJT CSI report<br>- Companies to report the modeling of CJT calibration report, including reporting periodicity, residual offset and fluctuation, all under realistic assumption<br>- Companies to report assumption of measurement accuracy error levels<br>- FFS on measurement RS requirement, e.g., frequency density and bandwidth, # of RS symbols, the gap between RS symbols<br>- Assumption on algorithms to determine delay/frequency/phase offset report | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For the study of explicit CSI feedback, the evaluation considers the following aspects:<br>- Form of explicit CSI<br>  - Opt-A: information facilitating NW reconstruction of the channel matrix H (e.g., NRX x NTX) per frequency granularity unit<br>    - Note:  NRX does not necessarily mean the number of UE Rx physical antenna(s)<br>  - Opt-B: information facilitating NW reconstruction of H’H per frequency granularity unit<br>  - Companies to report compression/quantization scheme used for the evaluation<br>- Handling of link adaptation via at least one of the following options<br>  - Opt-1: CQI/RI information in another report (e.g., based on CSI-RS or DMRS)<br>  - Opt-2: Assistance information (e.g., L1-SINR, L1-RSRP, Interference-plus-noise (IpN) information) in the same explicit CSI report or another report<br>  - Opt-3: the explicit feedback considering pre-whitening channel or channel covariance<br>  - Note: other options are not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | Draft LS R1-2606904 is endorsed in principle.<br>Final LS R1-2606905 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For evaluation on precoded CSI-RS without considering CSI-RS overhead reduction:<br>- Assume cell-specific wideband precoded CSI-RS, e.g., the number of precoded CSI-RS ports is same as the number of TXRUs at aNB<br>- Company reports the precoder of CSI-RS, and information/how to determinate the precoder.<br>- The following CSI feedback scheme can be considered for the evaluation:<br>- Option 1: Type I-like (W1W2) and/or eType II-like (W1W2Wf) CSI reporting with port-selection (PS) basis<br>- Option 2: Type I-like (W1W2) and/or eType II-like (W1W2Wf) CSI reporting with NR DFT basis with reconstruction information<br>- Option 3: Type I-like (W1W2) and/or eType II-like(W1W2Wf) CSI reporting with NR DFT basis (CSI-RS port precoder is transparent to UE)<br>- Company report how NW utilized the reported CSI for PDSCH precoder determination.<br>- FFS on other basis, e.g., the basis constructed from combined 2D DFT beams/ port-selection (PS) basis<br>- Benchmark:<br>  - Type I and eType II codebook with non-precoded CSI-RS<br>Companies report the QCL related assumptions<br>Note: Precoded CSI-RS with less density in spatial domain is to be discussed separately.<br>Note: downloadable basis/precoder (e.g. FD basis/precoder associated with each precoded CSI-RS port) can be considered additionally with precoded CSI-RS and the assumption/consideration for downloadable basis is taken into account<br>Note: the PAPR issue of precoded CSI-RS is to be studied. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For the evaluation on the benefit of early CSI triggering/reporting, considering the following aspects:<br>- Potential scenarios/use cases:<br>  - UE transition from IDLE/INACTIVE to CONNECTED mode,<br>  - During activation of serving cells /carriers<br>  - Mobility<br>  - Note: Early beam report is to be discussed separately<br>  - Other use cases are not precluded.<br>- For each potential use case, studying details of<br>  - Report quantities for each use case<br>  - RS for measurement<br>  - Benchmark<br>  - KPI<br>- Note: Strive to minimize Msg 1 impact.<br>- Note: Both measured and predicted CSI can be considered, if appliable. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For the evaluation on PDSCH DMRS-based CSI for PDSCH link adaptation, the following is consideration:<br>- For channel measurement, the DMRS port(s) associated with data is considered as the baseline<br>  - FFS on whether to consider DMRS port(s) allocated with PDSCH but not associated with the data<br>- For interference measurement (when needed), company to report how to acquire interference information.<br>- Company to report the UE assumptions for DMRS-based CSI calculation.<br>- Email discussion for the templates and collect results for the use cases, e.g., fixed codebook, AI-CSI, CPI, downloadable/configurable codebook/basis, DMRS-based CSI, precoded CSI-RS. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |

## 10.5.3.2 Aspects of uplink-based CSI acquisition

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.5 Multi-antenna system › 10.5.3 CSI acquisition and report

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For SRS, at least cyclic-shift and/or comb-offset are supported. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | Study whether/how TD-OCC can be supported together with SRS repetition to enable multiplexing of SRS ports, and at least take the following aspects into consideration:<br>- OCC lengths: e.g., TD-OCC2, TD-OCC4, other lengths<br>- Whether it is applicable to intra- and inter-UE multiplexing of SRS ports<br>- TD-OCC related interference randomization scheme<br>- The non-orthogonality incurred by partial dropping<br>- Channel variation in time domain | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For at least increasing SRS capacity/reducing overhead/improve coverage purposes sparse SRS patterns with comb values larger than 8, study at least comb 12 and 16 with consideration of at least the following aspects.<br>  - SRS sequences<br>  - MPR and PAPR/CM impact<br>  - Max number of cyclic shifts for the corresponding comb value<br>  - Minimum available bandwidth and its corresponding sequence<br>  - Channel estimation performance<br>  - Applicable RB sizes (e.g., RB size >= 8)<br>  - Any other performance impacts<br>- FFS other comb values.<br>- Note: RAN4 input on maximum power boosting is needed | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | Support cross-slot SRS transmission at least for consecutive S+U slots<br>- FFS: whether to support cross-slot SRS transmission spanning any two consecutive slots, e.g., U+U slots. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | Study at least the following aspects of precoded SRS<br>- Intended usage (e.g., for UL CSI and/or DL CSI)<br>- Feasibility under specific UE architectures (e.g., xTyR configurations for DL CSI)<br>- Gain compared to non-precoded SRS, accounting for practical impairments (e.g., PAPR/CM loss, realistic UE reciprocity, and UE coherency assumptions)<br>- Methods for precoder derivation of precoded SRS:<br>- Method-1: Determined by NW (e.g., derived from the measurements of SRS and indications from NW)<br>- Method-2: Determined (with/without reported) by UE via DL measurement<br>- FFS: UE UL/DL reciprocity calibration | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For SRS of 6GR, study the following frequency hopping aspects<br>- A single-level frequency hopping<br>  - hopping over bandwidth with one or multiple frequency hops<br>- Two-level frequency hopping<br>  - First level: hopping over BW with one or multiple frequency hops<br>  - Second level: hopping within each frequency hop of the first-level hopping<br>- For single or two levels, consider at least the following options<br>  - Nested tree-like frequency hopping pattern<br>  - Sequential hopping pattern<br>  - Pattern following RB offset provided by NW<br>  - With/without Intra-repetition hopping in the second level<br>Note: the case with larger DL BW than UL BW will be discussed separately | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | To support maximum SRS bandwidth beyond 272 PRBs in a carrier, consider the following options:<br>- Option 1: Table based approach (similar to NR)<br>  - Option 1-1:<br>    - Refer to the bandwidth entries for up to 272 PRBs in the table from NR<br>    - Introduce new SRS bandwidth entries from 272 to [544] PRBs<br>  - Option 1-2:<br>    - Scale up the SRS bandwidth entries in the table from NR by integer factor, e.g. value of 2 or 4<br>  - Option 1-3:<br>    - The overall SRS bandwidth is divided into more than one part, in which each part can reuse SRS bandwidth entries in the table from NR.<br>- Option 2: Flexible SRS bandwidth determination and frequency hopping<br>  - Replace the existing table-based SRS bandwidth determination with parameter-based frequency hopping pattern determination provided by NW | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |

FFS: Whether periodic RS for finer time and frequency tracking can be used in idle mode<br>    - Note: Periodicities of SSB will not be discussed in this agenda<br>  - Aperiodic RS<br>    - Conditions under which aperiodic RS is used, e.g. long periodicities for periodic RS<br>    - FFS: Whether aperiodic RS is associated with another periodic or aperiodic RS<br>    - Following options are considered<br>      - Option 1: RS other than PDSCH DMRS or PDCCH DMRS<br>      - Option 2: PDSCH DMRS or PDCCH DMRS<br>      - Note: DMRS pattern/design will not be discussed in this agenda<br>  - Aperiodic RS may have the same or different structure/pattern from that of periodic RS<br>- Note 1: Whether and how to use SSB for finer time and frequency tracking is up to UE implementation<br>- Note 2: The agreed KPI in RAN1#124 along with RS overhead should be considered for the study of the time/frequency/spatial domain factors for the periodic and aperiodic RS for finer time and frequency tracking targeting the following use cases:<br>    - Single/Multi-TRP<br>    - Single/Multi-carrier<br>    - Larger DL channel BW, e.g., up to 400MHz.<br>- For both periodic and aperiodic RS, details on time/frequency/spatial domain factor are separately studied based on the agreed candidates in RAN1#124bis and RAN1#125 | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Other Aspects related to CSI]` |

| Agreement | For the study on requirement/functionality of DL RS for beam measurement, for inter-symbol NW/UE-side beam sweeping, the following is considered:<br>- Study the following time-domain behavior of DL RS for beam measurement: aperiodic, semi-persistent and periodic.<br>- At least support the functionality for UE to assume whether DL RSs are transmitted with same DL Tx spatial filter(s) or not.<br>- Note: Sync-signal design is not considered herein<br>- At least following aspects to be considered for DL RS for beam measurement:<br>  - UE complexity,<br>  - Energy efficiency,<br>  - RS overhead,<br>  - Measurement performance, and corresponding coverage.<br>  - Beam measurement/sweeping latency<br>  - Multiplexing with other data and RS | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | Regarding QCL framework,<br>- At least support the following QCL properties for DL:<br>  - Doppler shift, Doppler spread, average delay, delay spread, and spatial Rx parameter<br>  - FFS: Whether/how to introduce average gain as a QCL property.<br>  - FFS: Other QCL properties<br>- FFS: Where/how/when to use above QCL properties.<br>- FFS: Source QCL-RS | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | Regarding UE initiated beam management, at least the following procedure can be considered<br>- Event detection for triggering UL transmission<br>- UL transmission procedure<br>- NW response, if any, to UL transmission procedure<br>  - UE behavior, if any, after receiving NW response. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | Regarding UE initiated beam management, at least for a single-TRP operation, on event detection for triggering UL transmission, the following can be studied for Event-1 and Event-2.<br>- Event evaluation metric/threshold,<br>- Current/serving DL RS determination,<br>- Candidate DL RS identification, if applicable. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | For the study on beam measurement and report regarding DL beam measurement and report procedure on DL RS(s) for multi-TRP scenario(s), the following can be considered on Case-1:<br>- Regarding L1-RSRP calculation, NR L1-RSRP definition can be used as the starting point.<br>  - FFS: Whether any other extension of L1-RSRP definition is needed, e.g., TRP-specific or port-specific L1-RSRP.<br>- Regarding RS measurement requirements,<br>  - Study the need of a functionality of enabling UE to a) differentiate DL RSs from different TRPs within a cell, or b) differentiate DL RSs across cells for DL beam measurement and report.<br>- Regarding report content/format,<br>  - Information of identifying DL RS can be included in the report as a starting point, besides for L1-RSRP<br>  - FFS: the following report content can be studied:<br>    - N DL RSs per selected TRP or per measured TRP<br>    - M DL RSs across different TRPs<br>  - FFS: Whether/How to include the information of selected TRP in the report.<br>  - FFS: other metrics, e.g., L1-SINR.<br>- Note (Case-1): To identify/report the preferred N>1 DL beams per NW TRP/panel or across NW TRP(s)/panel(s) for supporting multi-TRP/panel transmission scheme(s).<br>- Note: Above TRPs may belong to a same cell or different cells.<br>- Note (for the study purpose): The above study at least considers the following transmission schemes: TDMed and DPS transmission scheme, if supported. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | For the study on spatial/temporal/frequency domain beam measurement/prediction (relevant to AI/ML or non-AI/advanced schemes), the following is considered:<br>- Regarding spatial/temporal domain DL Tx beam prediction and management on multi-TRP/cell(intra/inter-cell), at least support the case that the cells/TRPs corresponding to the predicted beam(s) (Set-A) are within the set of cells/TRPs corresponding to the measured beam(s) (Set-B).<br>  - FFS: Other cases that the case that at least one of the cells/TRPs corresponding to the predicted beam(s) (Set-A) are outside the set of cells/TRPs corresponding to the measured beam(s) (Set-B).<br>- Regarding frequency domain beam measurement/prediction (cross-frequency), at least support the collocated scenarios of source and target frequencies (using same height of NW antenna(s)).<br>  - FFS: Non-collocated scenarios of source and target frequencies.<br>- FFS: How to ensure the training and inference consistency for UE-side model, e.g., association ID.<br>- FFS: Whether/how to indicate the combination coefficient(s), W, between measured beams (set B) for computation of the target beams (set A), e.g., matrix-based approach (from NW to UE).<br>  - Predicted results of Set-A is derived based on W and measured results of Set-B<br>- FFS: Report content<br>  - Whether/how to report confidence level associated with reported beam, e.g., inaccurate, invalid prediction | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | - Study target usage/necessity and procedure of UE UL beam sweeping<br>- From RAN1 perspective, as a baseline, Tx beam of UL channel/RS may be determined according to DL-RS.<br>  - Note: UE beam correspondence is assumed RAN1<br>- Send an LS to RAN4 | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | Draft LS R1-2606909 is endorsed in principle.<br>Final LS R1-2606910 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | Regarding UE initiated beam management, on a single-TRP operation, on UL transmission procedure, the following can be studied for Event-1 and Event-2.<br>- Candidate-1a: First UL channel + Second UL channel<br>  - First channel (e.g., PUCCH, PRACH) is to at least notify event occurrence;<br>  - Second channel (e.g., PUSCH) is to carry a beam report<br>- Candidate-1b: First UL channel + SRS<br>  - First channel (e.g., PUCCH) is to at least notify event occurrence;<br>  - SRS can be used for NW DL-Tx/UL-Rx beam determination.<br>- Candidate-2a: First UL channel at least notifying event occurrence<br>  - First UL channel can be PUCCH, PUSCH, or PRACH.<br>- Candidate-2b: SRS used for NW DL-Tx/UL-Rx beam determination | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | For network-initiated beam reporting in 6GR,<br>- Study the following time-domain behavior of beam reporting: aperiodic, semi-persistent, periodic.<br>- Support DL RS identifier as another report quantity in a beam report, when applicable.<br>  - Note: DL RS may be measured or predicted by the UE. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | Regarding DL beam measurement and report procedure, for the study on NW determination of refined analog beam(s), including the feasibility and necessity, the following is considered:<br>- Potential use cases:<br>  - To improve beam-forming gain/throughput via generating NW refined analog beam(s) based on UE report which may be different from measured beams<br>- Studying the details of:<br>  - Benchmark: Companies report the benchmark in evaluation.<br>    - Benchmark #1 Beam selection from a set of measured beams<br>    - Benchmark #2 Beam selection from a set of measured beams plus candidate analog beams (for subsequent transmission)<br>  - RS measurement requirements<br>  - Potential report content (based on the measured beams):<br>  - Applicable frequency range<br>  - Others are not precluded.<br>- Note: NW implementation information is not disclosed. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | Regarding NW initiated beam indication, study transmission configuration indicator (TCI) framework involving both joint and separate DL/UL TCI state(s) for both single-TRP/cell/carrier and multi-TRP/cell/carrier operations<br>- Regarding joint-DL/UL TCI state(s), study joint-DL/UL TCI state(s) at least apply to PDSCH, and PUSCH;<br>  - FFS: what other target channel(s)/RS(s) which shall/can follow the TCI state, including CSI-RS, SRS, PDCCH, PUCCH and PRACH<br>- Regarding separate DL/UL TCI state, study the following<br>- DL-TCI state(s) at least apply to PDSCH;<br>  - FFS: The target channel(s)/RS(s) which shall/can follow the DL-TCI state, including CSI-RS, and/or PDCCH<br>- UL-TCI state(s) at least apply to PUSCH;<br>  - FFS: The target channel(s)/RS(s) which shall/can follow the UL-TCI state, including SRS, and PRACH, and/or PUCCH<br>- FFS: At least for joint-DL/UL TCI state/DL-TCI state, one state comprises at least one source QCL-RS, and each source QCL-RS is associated with at least one QCL property.<br>  - The joint-DL/UL TCI state can also used for determining UL TX beam<br>  - FFS: Other transmission parameter(s) for DL and UL associated with the TCI state (e.g., power control parameters, TAG, etc), besides for QCL properties.<br>- FFS: At least for UL-TCI state, one state comprises at least one reference signal, if applicable, for determining UL TX beam.<br>  - FFS: Other transmission parameter(s) for UL associated with the TCI state (e.g., power control parameters, TAG, etc), besides for UL TX beam.<br>Note: TCI state is for the purpose of discussion | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |

Details FFS.<br>- FFS: Padding/reservation<br>- FFS: need for truncation<br>- FFS: need for size determination during bandwidth adaptation.<br>- Regarding UE’s DCI monitoring size budget, for a single carrier operation,<br>  - FFS: the maximum number of container sizes for a UE to monitor (e.g., for BD purpose) is Xmax.<br>  - FFS: Whether the maximum number of container sizes for a UE to monitor (e.g., for BD purpose) for UE’s RNTI (e.g., C-RNTI) is Ymax.<br>  - FFS: whether the limitation is per slot or across slots<br>  - FFS: for multi-carrier cases<br>- FFS minimum container size.<br>- FFS maximum container size.<br>- FFS maximum number of container sizes for two stage DCI<br>- Note: Using a combination of the above alternatives for different use cases is not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606048](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606048.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |

## 10.5.4.3 HARQ related Aspects

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.5 Multi-antenna system › 10.5.4 Downlink control channel, scheduling and HARQ operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | - For the study of downlink HARQ-ACK feedback granularity smaller than TB-level and the corresponding mechanism, candidate options in RAN1#125 are categorized as follow.<br>- Category A (HARQ-ACK schemes):<br>  - NR CBG HARQ-ACK feedback as comparison baseline<br>  - XOR-based retransmission feedback<br>  - CB/CBG-level HARQ-ACK feedback of high-probability error patterns / codebook-based compressed feedback<br>  - 2D lattice-based CBs feedback<br>- Category B (feedback mechanisms/procedures may be applicable on top of a Category A scheme):<br>  - Non-uniform CB-to-CBG mapping feedback<br>  - Two-stage feedback<br>  - Mixture of TB-level and CBG-level feedback<br>  - Per-PDSCH on-demand request of CBG-level feedback<br>  - Enabling/disabling of CBG-level HARQ-ACK feedback<br>  - HARQ-ACK feedback enabling/disabling for some of CBGs<br>- At least a common set of evaluation assumptions are to be determined in this meeting for Category A.<br>- FFS same/additional evaluation assumptions are to be applied for Category B. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606797](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606797.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | In 6GR, study whether/how to address the case where the UE’s HARQ soft buffer is insufficient (e.g., all HARQ processes across all carriers/cells requiring retransmissions for soft combining simultaneously with maximum TBS), considering the possibility of the case and the different ways to address the case | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606798](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606798.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | - For study of 6G HARQ-ACK codebook based on NR Type-1 codebook as starting point, consider at least the following<br>  - Whether / how to reduce the codebook payload size<br>  - Whether / how to reduce codebook construction complexity<br>- For study of 6G HARQ-ACK codebook based on NR Type-2 codebook as starting point, consider at least the following<br>  - Whether/how to achieve additional robustness against the DCI(s) missing including last DCI(s) missing<br>  - Whether the counting / accumulating indication is confined within a cell or a set of cells<br>- For study of 6G HARQ-ACK codebook based on NR (e)Type 3 codebook as starting point, consider at least the following<br>  - Whether/how to achieve additional robustness against missing the DCI triggering the feedback<br>  - Whether / how to reduce codebook payload size<br>- FFS for each codebook type:<br>  - HARQ-ACK bit ordering<br>  - Determination of HARQ-ACK codebook payload size<br>  - For relaxing the need for tight coordination between schedulers, determine which codebook type(s) should be considered<br>  - Scheduling complexity<br>  - How to carry HARQ-ACK bits for a DCI that does not schedule a PDSCH, for at least (e)type 3 codebook<br>  - Whether/how to support standalone operation<br>- FFS: other approaches | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606799](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606799.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | For the study of downlink HARQ-ACK feedback granularity smaller than TB-level, the following common set of simulation assumptions are to be used for evaluating Category A HARQ-ACK schemes:<br>\| # \| Parameter \| Assumptions \|<br>\| 1 \| Evaluation methodology \| LLS / SLS(optional) with link adaptation \|<br>\| 2 \| Carrier frequency \| 4 GHz Optional: 7GHz \|<br>\| 3 \| Channel bandwidth \| 100MHz, SNR points reported by company Optional: 400MHz, SNR points reported by company \|<br>\| 4 \| Subcarrier spacing \| 30kHz \|<br>\| 5 \| Channel model \| TDL-A 30ns, CDL-C 100ns Optional: TDL-C 100ns, CDL-A 30ns, CDL-B 30ns \|<br>\| 6 \| UE speed / Doppler \| 3km/h Optional: 30km/h, 80km/h \|<br>\| 7 \| MIMO configuration \| 4Rx UE; Rank: company to report (fixed to 1, 2, 4 or adaptive rank) \|<br>\| 8 \| Receiver functions \| Realistic channel estimation Precoding and receiver type to be reported \|<br>\| 9 \| Link adaptation \| OLLA enabled Target BLER 10% Optional: Other target BLER value \|<br>\| 10 \| NR CBG HARQ-ACK feedback as comparison baseline \| NR range 2/4/6/8 Optional: 16 for 400MHz, and other value(s) \|<br>\| 11 \| Metrics \| (Average) feedback overhead per TB Ratio of retransmitted CB(s)/CBG(s) size to original TB size Impact on DL control overhead Optional: Spectrum efficiency / UE throughput / UE power consumption \|<br>Note: Companies are encouraged to report any other assumptions/metric (e.g., traffic model, cell throughput) used in their SLS | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606799](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606799.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |

The UE reports RSRP/RSSI for the strongest one and differential RSRP/RSSI for the other N-1.<br>  - Option 2: a bit-map reporting with each bit corresponding each measurement resource, and bit value = 1 indicating the RSRP/RSSI measurement is above a configured threshold.<br>  - Study the benefit of each reporting content.<br>- Further study the following additional reporting content:<br>  - UE reports the time information corresponding to the measurement resource with the largest RSRP/RSSI measurement. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |

How neighboring cell RRM measurement is performed by EE processing is up to companies’ report, including power consumption. L1 measurement is up to companies’ report.<br>Note 2: For traffic, FTP, XR, IM can be evaluated and traffic arrvival rate is up to companies’ report<br>Note 3: Assumed sleep state is up to company report.<br>Note 4: In this evaluation, no further power scaling based on the number of occupied SSB symbols within a slot for the measurement by EE processing<br>Note 5: If RLM/RRM measurement is not evaluated, please report.<br>Note 6: it’s up to companies’ report how the evaluated C-DRX different from NR C-DRX<br>Note 7: it’s up to companies’ report how the evaluated DCP different from NR DCP<br>Note 8: when comparing different cases, companies are encouraged to at least consider the same monitoring periodicity of 6GR DL WUS and C-DRX cycle. Other periodicities are up to companies’ report. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| Agreement | For collecting UE energy saving results for 6GR DL WUS:<br>- Endorse the template for collecting UE energy saving results in R1-2606878.<br>Email discussion for collecting UE energy saving results in RRC idle by 2nd Oct, including potential updates to the template if any.<br>Email discussion for collecting UE energy saving results in RRC connected by 6th Nov. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |

## 10.6.2 Uplink WUS and operation

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.6 WUS and operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | - For feasibility study of DS#1b, further study the following mechanisms for UE to acquire UL WUS configuration.<br>  - Option 1: Pre-configuration and/or pre-defined in specification<br>  - Option 2: Synchronization signal(s)<br>  - Option 3: PBCH including PBCH DMRS<br>  - Option 4: Broadcast PDSCH<br>  - Option 5: Common PDCCH<br>  - Note: Combination of above options can be considered.<br>  - At least the followings are considered for further study of the above options<br>    - NW configuration flexibility<br>    - NW and UE energy saving/consumption<br>    - UE complexity<br>    - Signaling overhead<br>    - Necessary payload by taking SIB26 for NR OD-SIB1 as the reference | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| Agreement | - For study of necessity for UL WUS, at least followings are considered as candidates of use cases.<br>  - For UEs in idle mode,<br>    - UE-initiated on-demand or adaptation procedures for one or multiple of the following signals/channels:<br>      - 6GR SS/PBCH<br>      - 6GR SIB1<br>      - 6GR PRACH<br>      - 6GR DL Reference Signal<br>  - For UEs in connected mode,<br>    - UE-initiated on-demand or adaptation procedures for one or multiple of the following signals/channels:<br>      - 6GR SS/PBCH<br>      - 6GR SIB1<br>      - 6GR PRACH<br>      - 6GR DL Reference Signal<br>    - UL data indication (including ‘no UL data’ indication)<br>    - Selection of PUSCH resource and/or PUSCH transmission parameter<br>    - UL WUS based measurement<br>    - Indication of new beam for BFR (if supported for 6GR)<br>- With consideration of at least the following aspects:<br>  - 6GR SSB/SIB1 periodicity/repetition<br>  - Coverage<br>  - UL WUS detection performance<br>  - NW and UE energy/power consumption<br>  - Latency<br>  - NW and UE complexity<br>  - Resource overhead<br>  - Necessity of UE-triggered operation compared to NW-triggered mechanisms<br>  - Which DS(s) are applicable to the above use cases<br>  - Interference aspects<br>  - Inter-node coordination<br>  - Overlapping with existing or other 6GR mechanisms that could play the same functionalities | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| Agreement | - For study of feasibility for UL WUS, followings are observed for DS#2a in addition to previous RAN1 agreement.<br>  - If UE transmits UL WUS to cell/carrier #2, and if cell/carrier #1 and cell/carrier #2 are non-co-located,<br>    - UE can determine UL WUS transmission power as the pre-defined TX power (e.g., maximum TX power) Further study<br>      - Whether/how to mitigate interference to the cell/carrier other than cell/carrier #2<br>      - Whether/how to handle the impact to UL WUS detection performance<br>      - Whether UE can determine path-loss estimation from cell/carrier #1 DL signals and power offset value provided by cell/carrier #1 when available/applicable.<br>        - Details on how to use path-loss estimation from cell/carrier #1 DL signals<br>    - UE can use wide/omni-directional beam or beam sweeping as UL WUS transmission beam. Further study<br>      - How to handle the impact of reduced cell coverage, when using wide/omni-directional beam.<br>      - How to determine narrow beam and whether/how to mitigate interference to the cell/carrier other than cell/carrier #2.<br>    - UE can use cell/carrier #1 SSB as timing reference for UL WUS transmission when cell/carrier #1 and cell/carrier #2 are synchronized when the timing can be maintained in UE, or when cell#1/carrier #1 provides underlay coverage for cell#2/carrier #2.<br>    - It is required to further study whether/how UE can use cell/carrier #1 SSB as timing reference for UL WUS transmission or not, when cell/carrier #1 and cell/carrier #2 are not synchronized, considering inter-cell distance, timing advance, etc.<br>    - This can cover the scenario where UE moves from cell/carrier #1 to cell/carrier #2 (where cell/carrier #1 may not provide underlay coverage for cell/carrier #2) and can maintain DL timing/frequency synchronization and UL WUS configuration for a certain period of time<br>      - It is required to further study the duration of the certain period of time with coordination with RAN4 as needed | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| Agreement | - RAN1 to study necessary information and required payload size for UL WUS configuration, considering at least the following information and its necessity<br>  - Time and frequency domain resource size and location of UL WUS<br>  - Mapping between 6GR synchronization signal and UL WUS occasion<br>  - Parameters to determine UL WUS transmission power<br>  - Parameters to determine UL WUS sequence/format<br>  - Parameters for UE monitoring of response to UL WUS<br>  - Cell access related information<br>  - Note: SIB26 for NR OD-SIB1 is taken as a reference<br>  - Note: The above does not preclude that some necessary information may be implicitly determined, without need of explicit payload | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |

## 10.6.3 WUS operation with other functionalities

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.6 WUS and operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | - For the study of any information carried in DL-WUS with or, if supported, without the wake-up indication, RAN1 to at least use the following common assessment criteria:<br>  - Complexity of the UE EE processing and NW’s impact (if any);<br>  - Coverage impact of the resulting payload;<br>  - DL (and, where applicable, UL) resource overhead;<br>  - Energy-saving benefit at both UE side and network side, and latency impact;<br>  - Overlapping with existing or other 6GR mechanisms that could carry the same information<br>  - Whether DL-WUS used for other functions is beneficial than the existing or other 6GR mechanisms<br>  - The agreed FAR/FDR/MDR for DL WUS are used as the threshold for FAR/FDR/MDR of the additional (non-wake-up) information and associated functionality. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-WUS operation with other functionalities]` |
| Agreement | - For UEs in RRC_IDLE / RRC_INACTIVE state, RAN1 to study at least the following candidate additional functions for DL-WUS:<br>  - Candidate #1: SI-modification and/or ETWS-CMAS notification carried via DL WUS.<br>  - Candidate #2: Use DL-WUS as a trigger or indication for on-demand and/or additional RS and/or common signals/channels, if supported.<br>  - Candidate #3: Use DL-WUS to indicate PF/PO resources or RACH occasions that can be used.<br>  - Candidate #4: Study DL WUS to trigger Downlink Small Data Transmission (DL SDT) in inactive state, if supported. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-WUS operation with other functionalities]` |

| Working Assumption | On the link budget template for 6GR NTN, the following values are used for S-band:<br>- “(28a) Atmospheric loss (dB)” = 0.1dB for LEO 30 degree elevation and 0.2dB for GEO 12.5 degree<br>- “(28b) Shadow fading margin (dB)” = 3dB<br>  - Note: Additional losses due to e.g. light indoor/in-vehicle penetration loss may be considered under APC.<br>- “(28c) Scintillation loss (dB)” = 2.2dB<br>  - This is for the evaluated elevation angles.<br>- “(28d) Polarization loss (dB)” = 3dB<br>  - Assumes SAN antenna is circularly polarized and UE antenna is linearly polarized<br>- “(4b-2) /  (11-b2) Scanning loss (dB)” is<br>  - Calculated based on elevation angle and antenna element pattern gain, e.g. ~4dB for 30 degree elevation angle in LEO 600, if the SAN antenna is modeled as an array.<br>    - NOTE: RAN1 might need to revisit the agreed antenna element pattern gain.<br>- For UEs with omni antennas (11a)/(4c) “Boresight Antenna gain” is set to<br>  - -5.5dB as baseline.<br>    - 0dB can be considered optionally.<br>- “(20) Receiver implementation margin (dB)” = 1dB<br>- “(27) Penetration margin” = 0dB<br>  - Note: Losses due to e.g. light indoor/in-vehicle penetration loss may be considered under APC.<br>- “(28e) Other losses”: is set to:<br>  - For outdoor UEs, 0dB<br>  - For UEs in adverse propagation condition (APC): [a value between 6dB and 15dB]<br>    - This value will be decided in the next meeting based on input from companies (values and associated scenarios).<br>    - APC is only evaluated for downlink broadcast channel / signals.<br>    - NOTE: UEs in adverse propagation conditions is per RAN guidance and is documented in TR 38.914 as 0% of users in Case 1 and 4% of users in Case 2.<br>NOTE: The study of a particular scenario for the link budget template does not imply that, if RAN1 determines a coverage gap for that scenario, it will be studied or specified how to close it. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606422](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606422.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| Agreement | In 6GR NTN, under the label of “physical layer aspects of multi-satellite operation”, the following sub-aspects are studied under “physical layer aspects related to inter-satellite interference management”<br>- Co-channel interference, taking into account characteristics of NTN channels (e.g. large and time-varying Doppler / timing offsets due to orbital dynamics)<br>  - At least for LEO same orbit.<br>  - FFS other parameters (e.g. elevation angle)<br>- NOTE: It is assumed that the UE is only connected to one satellite at a time. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606422](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606422.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |

| Conclusion | For Rel-20 6G ISAC performance evaluation, for baseline cases/combinations, it is clarified that ‘convex hull’ refers to ‘convex hull of the TRP deployment’ in Indoor Hotspot (Indoor office) and Indoor Factory evaluation assumptions. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605742](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605742.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |

| Agreement | For Rel-20 6G ISAC performance evaluation, for baseline cases/combinations of sensing AGV in Indoor Factory (SH), the following parameters are assumed for clutters.<br>\| Clutter density: \| 20% \|<br>\| Clutter height: \| 2 m \|<br>\| Clutter size: \| 10 m \| | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605742](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605742.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | For Rel-20 6G ISAC performance evaluation, for object detection and/or tracking, the MDP (missed detection probability) and the FAP type 2 (false alarm probability type 2) are calculated as follows:<br>- Missed detection probability<br>where,<br>-	 is the number of missed targets in the drop n, i.e., the true target not associated with any detected object.<br>-	 is the number of true targets in the drop n.<br>-	 is total number of drops with at least one target per drop.<br>FFS: same MDP definition as 5GA ISAC study should also be evaluated<br>- False alarm probability Type-2<br>where,<br>-	 is the number of detected objects but not associated with any true targets in the drop n.<br>-	 is the total number of detected objects in the drop n.<br>-	 is number of drops with at least one detected object.<br>FFS: same FAP type-2 definition as 5GA ISAC study should also be evaluated | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605742](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605742.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | For Rel-20 6G ISAC performance evaluation, for object detection and/or tracking, when data fusion from multiple sensing nodes/pairs is applied:<br>- Instead of using detected objects per sensing node/pair, the jointly determined object(s) fused from detected objects of multiple sensing nodes/pairs will be used for association between detected object(s) and true target(s), calculating MDP, FAP Type 2 and positioning/velocity accuracy.<br>- Companies to report the method/algorithm to obtain the jointly fused object(s) from detected objects of multiple sensing nodes/pairs. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605742](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605742.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | For Rel-20 6G ISAC evaluation on SAC (sensing assisted communication), companies are encouraged to evaluate and report at least one of the following:<br>- Scope 1: Evaluate performance of sensing measurement/result, which will be used to assist communication;<br>- Scope 2: Evaluate performance of communication, with and without assistance of sensing measurement/result. The sensing measurement/result to assist communication can be:<br>  - Ideal sensing measurement/result<br>  - Non-ideal sensing measurement/result, and company to report how to model error/offset of non-ideal factors for non-ideal sensing measurement/result<br>  - Real sensing measurement/result, which is obtained from simulation<br>  - Note: companies to report which is used in their simulation.<br>For Rel-20 6G ISAC evaluation on SAC, for Scope 2:<br>- The communication use case(s) in AI 10.8.2 are encouraged<br>- Companies to report whether the location and/or velocity information of communication UE and/or TRP is needed or not in their SAC algorithm/mechanism<br>  - Note: this does not imply specifying exchange of location and/or velocity information between UE and TRP<br>- Companies to report the time-frequency resource cost due to sensing for SAC and necessary details on the definition of the time-frequency resource cost based on TR38.765 as starting point<br>  - Comparison with benchmark and benchmark details should also be reported as well as other necessary details if any<br>- Companies are encouraged to use communication-related performance metrics as agreed in communication agendas<br>- Companies are encouraged to use baseline/benchmark mechanism as agreed in communication agendas for comparison; Use of state-of-the-art mechanism without assistance of sensing measurement/result for comparison is not precluded<br>- Companies are encouraged to use additional communication-related evaluation assumptions (if necessary but not discussed/agreed in AI 10.8.1) as agreed in communication agendas<br>Note: How to draw conclusion/observation is a separate discussion. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605743](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605743.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | For Rel-20 6G ISAC evaluation on SAC, for Scope 1, the following sensing measurement/results and corresponding performance metrics are considered:<br>- Delay/Doppler/angle/power/phase related information of the ISAC channel<br>  - Performance metric:<br>    - RMSE of delay of the path(s) used to assist communication<br>    - RMSE of Doppler the path(s) used to assist communication<br>    - RMSE of angles (one or more of AOD, ZOD, AOA, ZOA) of the path(s) used to assist communication<br>    - RMSE of power of the path(s) used to assist communication<br>      - FFS: details on the power of the path(s)<br>    - FFS: RMSE of phase of the path(s) used to assist communication<br>    - Companies to report how to select the path(s), how to derive RMSE from multiple paths and how such sensing measurement/result of the paths(s) is able to assist communication<br>    - Note: when evaluated, companies are not required to report every metric<br>- Enrivonment (large surface, wall) that reconstructed by sensing<br>  - Performance metric:<br>    - Mean value of absolute perpendicular distance from the sensed specular reflection point to the ground-truth plane<br>    - Mean value of absolute perpendicular distance from the ground truth specular reflection point to the reconstructed plane<br>    - Note: when evaluated, companies are not required to report every metric<br>  - EO type-2 should be modeled in the simulation in this case<br>Companies are not required to evaluate and report every sensing measurement/result.<br>Note: In Scope 1, the performance metrics for detecting object for the purpose of SAC are the same as those for object detection and/or tracking, and thus already included in Rel-20 6G ISAC object detection and/or tracking study.<br>Note 2: How to draw conclusion/observation is a separate discussion. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605743](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605743.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | For Rel-20 6G ISAC evaluation on SAC, for evaluation assumptions, the evaluation assumptions of baseline cases/combinations of object detection and/or tracking are reused as starting point. In addition, one or more of the following adjustment may be considered:<br>- Dropping of sensing target is optional<br>- In case reconstruction of enrivonment (large surface, wall that modeled by EO type-2) is evaluated, additional sensing capable UEs can be dropped for reconstructing environment<br>- Other adjustment is not precluded<br>Companies should report the adjustments (if any) with necessary details when reporting simulation results. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605743](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605743.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | Templates (excel files) in R1-2606863 are adopted to collect evaluation results of object detection and/or tracking in AI 10.8.1.<br>Additional tdoc file(s) will also be used to collect companies input in details, which may include but not limited to:<br>- Details on sensing RS/resource<br>- Details on method/algorithm for detection<br>- Details on method/algorithm for tracking<br>- Details on inter-site/intra-site interference modelling, if modelled<br>- Details on method/algorithm to select sensing STx-SRx pair(s)<br>- Details on method/algorithm to obtain the jointly fused object(s) from detected objects of multiple sensing nodes/pairs, if data fusion is applied<br>- Details on the sensing capable UE dropping<br>Note: Further revision is not precluded, depending on future discussion | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605743](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605743.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.8 ISAC

| Working Assumption | - The following representative use cases for sensing assisted communication is/are selected for study in Rel-20 ISAC<br>  - Sensing assisted beam management<br>  - [Sensing assisted CSI acquisition and reporting]<br>  - Note: companies can also study other use cases for sensing assisted communication<br>- Note: In AI10.8, physical layer design discussions are limited to sensing related aspects | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605585](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605585.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | In addition to theoretical characteristics analysis, companies are encouraged to do at least one of followings to evaluate RE pattern of sensing RS in 10.8.2<br>- Full SLS with or without cooperative sensing as 10.8.1 (Option 1 from RAN1#125) for sensing performance evaluation<br>- SLS with non-cooperative sensing (i.e., no fusion) (Option 2 from RAN1#125) at least for sensing measurement performance evaluation<br>      - LOS condition is always assumed for each STX-target-SRX link<br>- Simplified SLS with single STX and single SRX (Option 3 from RAN1#125) at least for sensing measurement performance evaluation<br>      - LOS condition is always assumed for each STX-target-SRX link<br>- Note: detailed modelling is up to company report | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605586](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605586.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | Study on a sensing RS resource consisting of one or multiple component RS.<br>- Study the following options for component RS<br>  - Component RS can be RS for communication is reused for sensing<br>  - Component RS can be enhanced RS for sensing, taking reuse of RS for communication as starting point<br>  - Component RS can be dedicated RS for sensing<br>- Note: The multiple component RS of a sensing RS resource can be of single or multiple RS types.<br>- Companies to provide necessity, theoretical analysis, performance evaluation and specification impacts on the above options and the sensing RS resource in next RAN1 meetings.<br>- Note: RE pattern of the sensing RS resource is union set of all REs of all component RS<br>- FFS: details of component RS | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605586](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605586.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | Study the following two options to generate a sensing RS resource<br>- Option 1: A sensing RS resource has single antenna port, which can be precoded or non-precoded<br>- Option 2: A sensing RS resource can have multiple antenna ports, which can be precoded or non-precoded<br>- Companies to provide necessity, theoretical analysis, performance evaluation and specification impacts on the above options in next RAN1 meetings.<br>- FFS impact on measurement reporting and assistance information | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605586](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605586.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Conclusion | - On UE-side measurement report, measurement Level A from NR ISAC is not considered for 6G ISAC study in Rel-20. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605587](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605587.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - The following metrics for a path are to be studied in 6G ISAC<br>  - Delay information defined per path<br>    - Option 1: Receive time difference between a path and a reference path, e.g., RSTD like<br>    - Option 2: Receive time difference between a path and a reference time, e.g., RTOA like<br>      - FFS reference time<br>    - Option 3: Receive time of a path minus a transmission time, e.g., Rx-Tx time difference like<br>      - FFS transmission time<br>  - Doppler information defined per path<br>    - FFS absolute or relative/differential Doppler information<br>  - Angle information defined per path<br>    - FFS absolute or relative/differential angle information, implicit/indirect angle information<br>    - FFS how to derive angle<br>  - RSRP/SINR/amplitude/phase defined per path<br>    - FFS how to derive phase<br>  - Quality/uncertainty/confidence defined per path<br>  - FFS ambiguity handling of delay/Doppler/angle of a path<br>  - Further discuss the additional metrics for a path<br>    - Micro-Doppler<br>    - RCS<br>    - Association among paths<br>    - Other metrics are not precluded<br>  - FFS reference path<br>  - FFS definition of path<br>- Note: the necessity, applicable sensing modes, achievable sensing performance, reporting aspects (e.g., overhead) of above metrics can be discussed separately | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605587](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605587.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - The following metrics of a scattering point or an object are to be studied in 6G ISAC from RAN1 perspective<br>  - 3D or 2D location<br>    - Option1: Absolute location<br>    - Option2: Relative location referred to a reference location<br>    - FFS reference location<br>  - 3D or 2D velocity<br>    - Option1: Measured true 3D/2D velocity<br>    - Option2: Scalar of radial velocity<br>  - Uncertainty/confidence<br>  - association of measurements of a scattering point/object<br>  - FFS ambiguity handling of delay/Doppler/angle of a scattering point<br>  - Further discuss the additional metrics for a scattering point/object<br>    - Power related information if applicable, e.g., RCS<br>    - Micro-Doppler<br>    - Other metrics are not precluded<br>  - FFS definition of scattering point/object<br>- Note: the necessity, applicable sensing modes, and achievable sensing performance, reporting aspects (e.g., overhead) of above metrics can be discussed separately | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605587](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605587.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - The metrics of consecutive samples in delay/Doppler/angle profiles are to be studied in 6G ISAC<br>  - FFS: whether/how to apply window(s) of consecutive samples in delay/Doppler/angle profile<br>- Note: the necessity, applicable sensing modes, and achievable sensing performance, reporting aspects (e.g., overhead) of above metrics can be discussed separately | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605588](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605588.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |

## 10.8.3 Waveform and frame structure

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.8 ISAC

| Agreement | The following options are further studied for RS sequence enhancement for sensing based on ZC sequence based on CP-OFDM.<br>- Opt. 1 ZC sequence optimization<br>- Opt. 2 Frequency domain quadratic-phase RS sequence .<br>  - FFS: details of B, C, D<br>  - FFS: A as constant or whether/how to define A as function of m<br>    - Note: intention of A(m) here is different from FDSS<br>- [Opt. 3 Time domain RS sequence generation based on transform precoding]<br>- Opt. 4 Time domain spanning ZC sequence across OFDM symbols<br>RS sequence enhancement should identify:<br>- the benefits/drawbacks based on comprehensive comparison with NR Gold sequence for TRP transmission and NR ZC sequence for UE transmission as benchmark for evaluation<br>- applicability/necessity to TRP-transmission based sensing<br>- applicability/necessity to UE-transmission based sensing<br>Discussion on the other solutions will continue. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606564](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606564.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-ISAC-Waveform and FS]` |

| Agreement | Introduce 15 kHz NCP as an optional configuration for MBSFN subframes of a dedicated MBMS carrier in Rel-20 TEI.<br>Note 1: Assuming UE operating in Receive Only Mode (ROM), no MDT support and no UE capability reporting required.<br>Note 2: The related CR work could then be discussed and completed during 2026 Q4 WG meetings.<br>Note 3: it is assumed that 15kHz NCP will be deployed with a single cell.<br>Note 4: it is assumed that if the cell has an MBSFN area with 15 kHz NCP, no other MBSFN area of the same cell has a different numerology on the same frequency carrier. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606848](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606848.zip)<br>`[126-R20-TEI]` |
